# 20. OpenShift AI component overview
> **Track:** OpenShift AI Fundamentals — Corporate/L3
> **Audience:** Senior OpenShift administrators, platform engineers, MLOps engineers, SREs, security engineers, architects, and technical leads
> **Version basis:** Aligned to the Red Hat OpenShift AI Self-Managed 3.5 documentation stream available in July 2026. Always confirm support matrices, API stability, operator channels, and component status for your exact subscription and cluster release.
> **Hands-on project:** Inventory, enable, validate, and operate OpenShift AI components
---
## Executive definition
OpenShift AI components span operator management, dashboard, workbenches, pipelines, model serving, registries, catalogs, distributed workloads, scheduling, RAG/OGX, storage integrations, networking, and observability.
This module treats the subject as an enterprise platform capability rather than a product-demo feature. The emphasis is architecture, ownership, controls, operability, failure handling, repeatability, and measurable production outcomes.
## Learning outcomes
- Explain every component.
- Validate component health.
- Map dependencies.
- Create operational runbooks.
- Translate the topic into architecture diagrams, operational procedures, security controls, and acceptance tests.
- Explain trade-offs to application owners, data scientists, auditors, and senior management.
- Diagnose failures using a layer-by-layer and evidence-driven troubleshooting method.
## Scope and assumptions
- A supported Red Hat OpenShift cluster is available or planned.
- The OpenShift AI subscription, operator channel, and support matrix are validated before implementation.
- Identity, DNS, certificates, storage classes, ingress, registry access, and monitoring are treated as shared platform dependencies.
- GPU or alternative accelerator capacity is optional for fundamentals but mandatory for many production training and large-model inference workloads.
- S3-compatible object storage is planned for datasets, pipeline artifacts, model artifacts, and other lifecycle data when required.
- Production changes are delivered through Git, peer review, automated validation, and controlled promotion.
- Examples use placeholders and must not be copied into production without security review.
- Feature status can differ between General Availability, Technology Preview, Developer Preview, and community upstream implementations.

## Component relationship map
### Component 1: OpenShift AI Operator
- **Purpose:** Describe why OpenShift AI Operator is relevant to OpenShift AI component overview and which user or controller consumes it.
- **Inputs:** Identify configuration, credentials, data, artifacts, quotas, network access, and dependencies required by OpenShift AI Operator.
- **Outputs:** Record APIs, artifacts, logs, metrics, status conditions, and user-visible outcomes produced by OpenShift AI Operator.
- **Dependencies:** Map OpenShift APIs, storage, DNS, certificates, identity, Operators, and external services used by OpenShift AI Operator.
- **Security:** Define service accounts, RBAC, secrets, network policy, image trust, audit, and data classification for OpenShift AI Operator.
- **Reliability:** Define health checks, replica strategy, disruption controls, backup scope, recovery objective, and alerting for OpenShift AI Operator.
- **Operations:** Establish installation, configuration, upgrade, troubleshooting, support, and decommission procedures for OpenShift AI Operator.

## Reference manifests and command patterns

> These examples are instructional baselines. Validate API versions and component support on your exact OpenShift AI release.

### Project namespace

```yaml
apiVersion: project.openshift.io/v1
kind: Project
metadata:
  name: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/part-of: openshift-ai-fundamentals
    training.redhat.com/topic: "rhoai-component-overview"
    environment: lab
    owner: ai-platform-team
```

### ResourceQuota baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ResourceQuota
metadata:
  name: ai-team-quota
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  hard:
    requests.cpu: "8"
    requests.memory: 32Gi
    limits.cpu: "16"
    limits.memory: 64Gi
    requests.storage: 500Gi
    persistentvolumeclaims: "10"
    count/pods: "50"
```

### LimitRange baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: LimitRange
metadata:
  name: ai-team-defaults
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  limits:
    - type: Container
      defaultRequest:
        cpu: "250m"
        memory: 512Mi
      default:
        cpu: "2"
        memory: 4Gi
```

### Default-deny NetworkPolicy

```yaml
apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: NetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: default-deny
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  podSelector: {}
  policyTypes:
    - Ingress
    - Egress
```

### ServiceAccount for controlled automation

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ServiceAccount
metadata:
  name: ai-pipeline-runner
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
automountServiceAccountToken: true
```

### Role for project-level read operations

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: Role
metadata:
  name: ai-project-reader
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
rules:
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["pods", "services", "configmaps", "persistentvolumeclaims"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
  - apiGroups: ["apps"]
    resources: ["deployments", "statefulsets"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
```

### RoleBinding

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: RoleBinding
metadata:
  name: ai-project-readers
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
subjects:
  - kind: Group
    name: ai-data-scientists
    apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
roleRef:
  kind: Role
  name: ai-project-reader
  apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
```

### S3-compatible data connection secret pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: Secret
metadata:
  name: lab-object-storage
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
    opendatahub.io/managed: "true"
  annotations:
    openshift.io/display-name: Lab object storage
type: Opaque
stringData:
  AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_S3_ENDPOINT: https://s3.example.internal
  AWS_S3_BUCKET: ai-lab
  AWS_DEFAULT_REGION: us-east-1
```

### GitOps application pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
kind: Application
metadata:
  name: rhoai-component-overview
  namespace: openshift-gitops
spec:
  project: default
  source:
    repoURL: https://git.example.internal/ai-platform/fundamentals.git
    targetRevision: main
    path: topics/rhoai-component-overview
  destination:
    server: https://kubernetes.default.svc
    namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  syncPolicy:
    automated:
      prune: true
      selfHeal: true
    syncOptions:
      - CreateNamespace=true
```

### Baseline validation commands

```bash
oc whoami
oc version
oc get clusterversion
oc get csv -A | grep -i -E 'rhods|opendatahub|nvidia|kueue|serverless|servicemesh'
oc get datasciencecluster -A -o wide
oc get dscinitialization -A -o wide
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-operator
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get routes -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp | tail -100
```
## From-scratch corporate project
### Project goal: Inventory, enable, validate, and operate OpenShift AI components
### Phase 1: Business framing and success criteria
**Objective:** Apply business framing and success criteria specifically to **OpenShift AI component overview**.
1. Define the deliverable and an accountable owner for OpenShift AI Operator, with explicit consideration of component purpose.
2. Record assumptions and unresolved questions for DSCInitialization, with explicit consideration of dependencies.
3. Create objective entry and exit criteria for DataScienceCluster, with explicit consideration of CRDs.
4. Identify dependencies and support boundaries for dashboard, with explicit consideration of namespaces.
5. Capture security and compliance requirements for workbenches, with explicit consideration of status.
6. Define evidence retained for audit for Data Science Pipelines, with explicit consideration of data flows.
7. Write an automation or gitops approach for KServe, with explicit consideration of security.
8. Define validation commands and expected results for model registry, with explicit consideration of scaling.
9. Document failure modes and rollback for model catalog, with explicit consideration of upgrades.
10. Review capacity, cost, and licensing impact for Kueue, with explicit consideration of troubleshooting.
11. Peer-review the design with platform and application teams for distributed workloads, with explicit consideration of component purpose.
12. Obtain change approval before production for OGX, with explicit consideration of dependencies.
**Phase evidence:**
- Approved record for business framing and success criteria.
- Configuration or design artifact stored in Git for rhoai-component-overview.
- Test output showing both successful and intentionally failed paths.
- Named owner and escalation path for unresolved risk.

## Detailed hands-on lab sequence
### Lab 1: Collect cluster and operator inventory
**Goal:** Collect cluster and operator inventory for OpenShift AI component overview, using OpenShift AI Operator as the primary observation point.
**Procedure:**
1. Perform step 1 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate component purpose, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
2. Perform step 2 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate dependencies, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
3. Perform step 3 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate CRDs, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
4. Perform step 4 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate namespaces, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
5. Perform step 5 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate status, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
6. Perform step 6 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate data flows, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
7. Perform step 7 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate security, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
8. Perform step 8 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate scaling, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
**Expected result:**
- The OpenShift AI Operator state is observable and consistent with the declared configuration.
- Access is restricted to the intended identity and project boundary.
- Logs, events, metrics, and status conditions provide enough evidence for operations.
- Repeating the procedure produces the same result without undocumented manual changes.
**Failure injection:**
- Temporarily introduce a safe lab-only misconfiguration related to dependencies.
- Record the symptom, first failing layer, root cause, corrective action, and prevention control.
**Cleanup:**
- Restore the declared configuration from Git and verify reconciliation.
- Remove temporary credentials, test artifacts, and elevated access.
### Lab 2: Validate identity and group mappings
**Goal:** Validate identity and group mappings for OpenShift AI component overview, using DSCInitialization as the primary observation point.
**Procedure:**
1. Perform step 1 for validate identity and group mappings; validate dependencies, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
2. Perform step 2 for validate identity and group mappings; validate CRDs, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
3. Perform step 3 for validate identity and group mappings; validate namespaces, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
4. Perform step 4 for validate identity and group mappings; validate status, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
5. Perform step 5 for validate identity and group mappings; validate data flows, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
6. Perform step 6 for validate identity and group mappings; validate security, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
7. Perform step 7 for validate identity and group mappings; validate scaling, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
8. Perform step 8 for validate identity and group mappings; validate upgrades, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
**Expected result:**
- The DSCInitialization state is observable and consistent with the declared configuration.
- Access is restricted to the intended identity and project boundary.
- Logs, events, metrics, and status conditions provide enough evidence for operations.
- Repeating the procedure produces the same result without undocumented manual changes.
**Failure injection:**
- Temporarily introduce a safe lab-only misconfiguration related to CRDs.
- Record the symptom, first failing layer, root cause, corrective action, and prevention control.
**Cleanup:**
- Restore the declared configuration from Git and verify reconciliation.

## Enterprise control catalogue
### Control 1: Identity And Access
- **Policy statement:** OpenShift AI component overview must implement documented identity and access controls for OpenShift AI Operator.
- **Design requirement:** The control must account for CRDs, tenant boundaries, and version-specific support constraints.
- **Implementation evidence:** Git commit, manifest, configuration export, approval record, and automated test result.
- **Operational evidence:** Metrics, logs, alerts, access review, capacity report, incident record, or restore test as applicable.
- **Failure criterion:** The control fails when unauthorized, untraceable, unsupported, unmonitored, or unrecoverable behavior is possible.
- **Review frequency:** Review after material platform changes and on the organization's scheduled control cycle.

## SRE and operational readiness
### Reliability objective 1: Dashboard Availability
- **Indicator:** Define how dashboard availability is measured for OpenShift AI component overview.
- **Objective:** Select a target from business criticality, error budget, dependency reliability, and support capability.
- **Telemetry:** Identify source metrics, log queries, events, synthetic tests, and dashboard panels.
- **Alert:** Alert only when user impact or error-budget risk requires action.
- **Runbook:** Include diagnosis, mitigation, escalation, rollback, and post-recovery validation.
- **Review:** Evaluate trend, saturation, false positives, and improvement actions during service review.

## Troubleshooting playbook
### Scenario 1: Dashboard Is Unavailable
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating OpenShift AI component overview and is first observed around workbenches.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Taint, toleration, affinity, or node-selector mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get pods -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get subscriptions.operators.coreos.com -A`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass dependencies controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 2: User Cannot See A Project
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating OpenShift AI component overview and is first observed around Kueue.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Artifact metadata mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get csv -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass CRDs controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 3: Workbench Remains Pending
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating OpenShift AI component overview and is first observed around DataScienceCluster.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Data distribution or quality shift.
- **First command:** `oc get subscriptions.operators.coreos.com -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get storageclass`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass namespaces controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.

- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating OpenShift AI component overview and is first observed around model registry.

- **First command:** `oc get datasciencecluster -A -o yaml`
- **Second command:** `oc logs <pod> -n <namespace> --all-containers`

## Production design decisions
### Decision 1: Dedicated Versus Shared Openshift Cluster
- **Question:** Which dedicated versus shared OpenShift cluster pattern best supports OpenShift AI component overview?
- **Option A:** Optimizes centralized consistency, reuse, governance, and operational efficiency.
- **Option B:** Optimizes isolation, autonomy, locality, or specialized performance.
- **Selection criteria:** Business criticality, data location, latency, scale, skills, security, availability, cost, and supportability.
- **Evidence:** Benchmark results, threat model, cost model, failure test, and stakeholder approval.
- **Revisit trigger:** Material workload growth, new regulation, new supported feature, incident trend, or architecture change.

## Expanded glossary
### Term 1: AI platform
- **Definition:** A managed set of services, controls, and workflows for creating and operating AI solutions.
- **Relevance:** In OpenShift AI component overview, relate AI platform to OpenShift AI Operator, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 2: MLOps
- **Definition:** Practices that automate and govern the machine-learning lifecycle.
- **Relevance:** In OpenShift AI component overview, relate MLOps to DSCInitialization, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 3: GenAIOps
- **Definition:** Operational practices for foundation-model, RAG, prompt, agent, evaluation, and inference lifecycles.
- **Relevance:** In OpenShift AI component overview, relate GenAIOps to DataScienceCluster, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 4: ModelOps
- **Definition:** Portfolio-wide governance and operations for models, versions, approvals, deployments, and risk.
- **Relevance:** In OpenShift AI component overview, relate ModelOps to dashboard, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 5: Workbench
- **Definition:** A project-scoped interactive development environment such as JupyterLab or code-server.
- **Relevance:** In OpenShift AI component overview, relate Workbench to workbenches, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 6: Pipeline
- **Definition:** A repeatable workflow of containerized tasks for data and model lifecycle automation.
- **Relevance:** In OpenShift AI component overview, relate Pipeline to Data Science Pipelines, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 7: Model registry
- **Definition:** A governed metadata and artifact catalog for model versions and lifecycle state.
- **Relevance:** In OpenShift AI component overview, relate Model registry to KServe, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 8: Model catalog

## Final production-readiness statement
A production implementation of **OpenShift AI component overview** is ready only when functionality, security, reliability, performance, supportability, cost, auditability, and recovery have objective evidence. A successful demonstration is not equivalent to an operationally supported enterprise service.
